# BUSBAR 1.6.0 — THE LEDGER

**There are exactly two documents in this release.**

| file | answers | authority |
|---|---|---|
| `BUSBAR-1.6.0.md` | *How is this built?* — Laws, the **85** locked decisions, the seams | **THE SPEC.** Part 1 > Part 3 > Part 2 > Part 5 |
| `1.6.0-LEDGER.md` (this file) | *What is left, and who is on it?* | **THE WORKLIST.** One row per open item |

Everything else under `docs/design/1.6.0-*.md` is **SCRATCH**: an audit writes it, its rows
are folded in here, and **the scratch file is then DELETED**. A scratch file that survives its
fold becomes tomorrow's stale doc that misleads the next agent — that is the disease this
structure exists to cure. Twenty parallel documents is how a release stops knowing what it is.

**ONE EXCEPTION, MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED — `1.6.0-TRACKER.md` IS NOT SCRATCH.** It is read by a
gate at run time: `xtask/src/gates/no_deferral.rs:60` pins it as `const TRACKER`, `:310` fails the
`no-deferral:waiver-shape` row outright if it cannot be read, and `:346` requires every waiver's
`expiry` id to resolve to a row **in that file**. Deleting it turns the no-deferral gate red on
every commit. It was listed in THE FOLD in error; it is struck from that list below and stays.

asserted — is GATE-READ.** Measured from the gates, scripts, workflows and crates themselves.

**EVERY DOCUMENT THIS LEDGER HAS DELETED WAS CHECKED AGAINST THIS REGISTER FIRST, AND NONE IS ON IT.**

## THE ROW SCHEMA

```
ID | ITEM | WHERE | CLASS | STATUS | OWNER | EVIDENCE
```

* **CLASS** — `money` · `security` · `gate` · `test` · `doc` · `branch` · `engine`
* **STATUS** — `OPEN` · `IN-FLIGHT` · `DONE` · `PARKED-OWNER` · `DEAD`
* **EVIDENCE** — a command, a commit, or a file:line. Never a claim without one.

Ordering is by CLASS: **money first, then security**, then the rest. That is the Laws' order
and it is not negotiable.

### Three rules for every row

1. **A row without EVIDENCE is not a row.** "Looks fine" is not evidence; a command someone
   else can re-run is.
2. **A gap and a failure must never be the same output — and neither may a gap and a success.**
   If a check could not run, its row says `ABSENT`, never `DONE`.
3. **Never self-approve a billed-byte change (#10/#59).** Money defects go to `PARKED-OWNER`
   with cell, diff, root cause and recommendation. The owner signs, not the agent.

### A fourth rule, for this file only: **a copied claim stays a copied claim**

Rows folded from an audit carry that audit's evidence, not this file's endorsement. Where two
audits contradict each other **both** are recorded and the contradiction is flagged in the row.
Where an audit asserted something with no evidence, the row says `EVIDENCE: none given` rather
than inheriting the assertion as fact.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **O9** | **THE ORACLE'S PER-PUSH CI JOB EXITS 0 ON A DIVERGENCE — the job is literally named "0 divergences" and it cannot fail.** `strict` defaults to **false**, and `bin/oracle`'s replay forwards to `diff` without it. **MEASURED: an unfiltered run printed 913 FAIL rows and still exited 0.** `manual-keep-proof.yml` and the turnstile **do** pass `--strict`; the two automated jobs do not. **WHY THIS OUTRANKS EVERYTHING ELSE IN THIS FILE:** the oracle is the never-waived safety net (#10). **Every other row in this ledger that rests on "the oracle would catch it" rests on a gate that cannot go red.** Read with S8 (59.5% of owed cells pre-authorised) and S9 (accepted-gaps structurally unreachable), the honest statement is: **only 39.5% of the corpus is ever compared, and even that is advisory.** **DETECTION IS FINE — ENFORCEMENT IS NOT**, and that distinction is the good news: a planted one-line 404 change was **CAUGHT, NAMED AND LOCATED** (`http.crosscut\|unknown-path\|bare FAIL headers,body headers content-length: '110' -> '121'`, siblings still PASS). **The fix is a flag, not an instrument rebuild.** | `.github/workflows/ci.yml:3175`; `bin/oracle` replay → `diff` | **PARKED-OWNER** | the 913-FAIL/exit-0 run. **DO NOT LET ANYONE BUT THE OWNER MARK THIS FIXED:** adding `--strict` turns a currently-green **required** gate red across the board, and that is a release-scheduling decision, not a code change |

### ⛔ CAN WE SHIP AT ALL? — **NO.** TWO AUDITS, TWO REPOSITORIES, TWO INDEPENDENT NOs

**busbar:** *"**No.** The path `predev → dev → qa → main` is broken in at least four independent
places, and the first break is upstream of every gate: **`predev` cannot run CI at all.**"*
*(⚠ the same document then enumerates **ten** blockers, B1–B10. "At least four" and the table's
ten are never reconciled — both recorded.)*

**busbar-release:** *"**No — and the engine is right to say no.** The headline is not that the
engine is broken. It builds clean, it is fmt-clean, and **334 of 334 tests pass.** The headline is
that the gate it exists to enforce is **red on the candidate**, and that several of the fixes that
would make it green **exist only in an uncommitted working tree or on an unmerged branch.**"*

### ☠ THE DOMINANT DEFECT CLASS OF THIS RELEASE — *an instrument that cannot produce a NO is not a check*

**This is not a collection of unrelated bugs. It is one defect, found eight times in one night, by
eight different audits that did not know about each other.** Every instance is an instrument that
RUNS, REPORTS, and CANNOT REACH THE VERDICT IT CLAIMS TO RENDER. Fixing them one at a time will
miss the ninth.

| # | the instrument | how it fails to say NO | row |
|--:|---|---|---|
| 1 | the shadow oracle's per-push CI job | printed **913 FAIL rows and exited 0** | **O9** |
| 2 | `xtask/src/audit.rs::confirmed()` | implements only the two-auditor half, not the consecutive-run half, so a scope going **`zero → findings → zero` reads CLEAN** | **G18** |
| 3 | `no-float-money` | scans four money files and **exempts the only one containing a float** | **G11** |
| 4 | the conformance TCK | **greens on zero MUSTs executed** | **R40** |
| 5 | `stub-idp.py`, the OIDC fleet probe | matches `startswith("/mint")`, so **`/mint/bad-signature` returns a valid, in-audience, unexpired token** | **S17** |
| 6 | the `accepted-gaps` register | `find_accept` is reachable only for ids in `owed-baseline.txt`; **none of its three cells is in that file** | **S9** |
| 7 | the 40-URL SSRF parity corpus | compares the two argument copies **to each other, and both are wrong together** | **S20** |
| 8 | `scrub_key`'s four copies | copy-vs-copy comparison returns *"consistent"* — **the copies agree, and both real ones are incomplete** | **S19** |

**Instances 7 and 8 are the sharpest, because they are the same trap in two subsystems: a
comparison between two implementations proves AGREEMENT, never CORRECTNESS.** Neither corpus can
see a defect both copies share. That is also exactly why M1's agreement test is tautological and
why S3's SSRF parity test *"was not a fix"*.

**THE SIBLING CLASS IS NOW PROVEN AND GATED, AND ITS COROLLARY EXPLAINS WHY IT HID FOR SO LONG:**

> **A capability that is never constructed is a capability that does not ship. Its tests prove the
> code works; they prove nothing about whether it RUNS.**
>
> **COROLLARY — A TEST SUITE CANNOT DETECT THIS CLASS, BECAUSE THE TEST CONSTRUCTS THE CAPABILITY
> ITSELF — THE ONE THING PRODUCTION DOES NOT DO.** More tests make it *less* visible, not more.

**36 findings; instrument shipped as `cargo xtask gate unconstructed` (G37).** G6 is that rule
(S6, M18, M23, R14, S19, M44, M45, S27, ENG27), and G10 — *a rule whose entries cannot
be reached is indistinguishable from an empty rule*. Counting all three families, **this shape
accounts for more rows in this ledger than any product defect.**


### ⚠ STANDING WARNING ON GATE EVIDENCE — READ BEFORE TRUSTING ANY ROW WHOSE PROOF IS "I RAN THE GATE"

**A GATE BINARY SCANS THE TREE IT WAS COMPILED IN, NOT THE ONE IT IS RUN IN.** `Ctx::workspace()`
bakes `env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")` in **at compile time**, so an `xtask` **copied into a worktree
scans the ORIGINAL checkout.** Measured: in one audit's first control round **every gate reported
green over plants it was never reading**; rebuilding `xtask` inside the worktree made the reds
appear. **It was caught only because the plants were grepped back.**

**THIS IS RETROACTIVE.** Any gate result obtained from a **copied or prebuilt** `xtask` in a
**worktree** is about the **wrong tree** and must be re-run. Known affected: at least one audit
tonight ran gates from a **16:06 prebuilt binary** because the working tree would not compile —
**its gate-derived numbers need re-confirmation; its TOML-parse numbers are unaffected.** Do not
silently trust any row whose evidence is *"I ran the gate in a scratch worktree."*

Carried as **G21**. Sibling of **GS24** (*a prebuilt `xtask` is only valid against the tree it was
built from*) — **that row predicted this one, from the config side.**

## MONEY (Part 1 — supreme; "it is right or it is wrong")

**66 rows — 47 `PARKED-OWNER`, 15 `OPEN`, 3 `IN-FLIGHT`, 1 context. 0 `DONE`, and that is deliberate: this ledger does not
close a money row. One (M5) is fixed in the tree and parked on the owner's signature, not on work.**

> **THE MONEY CENSUS'S VERDICT, STATED PLAINLY: IT WOULD NOT PASS A BANK AUDITOR.**
> **Read M20, M21, M22, M1, M23 and M27 first** — a computed price sealed into the WAL, a ~0.2% silent
> underbill on every configured rate in every deployment, a `settled` field declared in nano-units
> that carries bytes, a saturating cast with a measured `u64::MAX`-vs-`0` split, and a signed
> back-dated correction that moves the invoice and never the book. Everything else in this table is
> real and none of it is that.
>
> **One auditor's process note, worth more than any single row: it initially judged M20 LAWFUL and
> reversed only after following the arithmetic through four hops.** A field-name census passes
> M20. That is the standard this section is held to.
Money is binary and never self-approved. No agent closes a row in this section.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | **An absurd-but-finite rate saturates to `u64::MAX` instead of being refused.** The guard's own comment claims it ensures "never a huge/garbage integer rate"; it does not. Rust float→int casts saturate, so `1e30` passes `is_finite() && v > 0.0` and becomes `u64::MAX` nanos/token. A config typo becomes a catastrophic overcharge. **⚠ A REPORT THAT THE DEFECT HAS TWO HOMES IS WRONG, AND THE COLLATOR MEASURED IT.** One audit reported a second identical defect at `crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs:208`. Re-measured at HEAD: **`:208` is a CALL SITE inside `from_micro_rates_in`, not a second definition**, and the real definition at `rate.rs:40-47` **HAS the clamp** — `if v.is_finite() && v > 0.0 && v <= u64::MAX as f64`. **The drift runs the other way, and that is worse:** the fix exists, it landed on the ledger copy, and it was never carried to the one on the enforcement path. A third audit says the same and names the incident — `crates/busbar-kernel-budget/src/price.rs:59-74` writes it down: *"a clamp for a finite-but-overflowing rate went onto one copy and not the other, and the two answered a config typo with too many zeros as 'nothing' on one side and 'the largest rate there is' on the other."* The budget crate was rewired onto `nano_rate`; **`cost.rs` was not.** **AND THE AGREEMENT TEST IS TAUTOLOGICAL** — it compares `nano_rate` against a `RateNanos` that now delegates to `nano_rate`, and never sees the kernel's copy. So a rate-card typo yields `u64::MAX` nanos/token in the enforcement pricer and `0` in the ledger, and no test can see the disagreement. **MEASURED CONSEQUENCE, no longer hypothetical: at `1e17` µ/unit the door reads `u64::MAX` and the book reads `0`** — verbatim the drift `kernel-budget` claims to have eliminated. | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs:138-145`, reached in production at `cost.rs:446`. **NOT** `busbar-kernel-ledger` — that copy is correct | **PARKED-OWNER** | `fn nanos(utok: f64) -> u64 { let v = (utok*1000.0).round(); if v.is_finite() && v > 0.0 { v as u64 } else { 0 } }` at `cost.rs:138`; `nano_rate` at `rate.rs:40-47` **with** `&& v <= u64::MAX as f64` — re-read by the collator with `sed -n '35,55p' crates/busbar-kernel-ledger/src/cost/rate.rs`, and `sed -n '195,225p'` shows `:208` is `.set(currency, nano_rate(micro))`, a call. Re-checked against the tree the same day by a third audit: *"`crates/busbar-kernel/src/cost.rs` `fn nanos` — still no `v <= u64::MAX as f64`. OPEN."* No magnitude bound exists upstream: `RawTierRates` (`crates/busbar-substrate-values/src/billing.rs:171-180`) carries bare `f64` per tier into core |

## GATE — instruments, rules and registers

**98 rows — 81 `OPEN`, 3 `DONE`, 6 `PARKED-OWNER`, 1 `IN-FLIGHT`, 6 standing practice, 2 context.**
G1–G5 are properties of the register + judge that make the money rows
above cheaper to create and harder to see; none is an entry defect. G6–G11 are instrument defects
found by other audits. **G6 and G10 are the same disease in two costumes** — a capability that is
never constructed, and a rule whose entries can never be reached. Both report success for having
done nothing, and between them they account for six separate findings in this ledger.

| ID | ITEM | WHERE | STATUS | EVIDENCE |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G1 | **The register has ONE `by:` field and edits accrete under it — TEN widenings have landed under an unchanged signature.** There is no per-edit provenance, so an agent's widening reads as the owner's ruling (M7 is the money instance; `2b366e354` claims a "re-stamp" of a signature that is byte-identical before and after). Recommendation: an append-only `amendments: [{date, by, what}]` array, plus a gate that reds a `classes` widening whose newest amendment is not the owner's. | `accepted-differences.json` schema | **OPEN** | `48ea84225` widened `G-1.classes` with `by:` unchanged. Since 2026-09-15 five entries were removed by **fixing** the divergence (`D-2`, two `F-013`s, `F-017`, `O-1`, all in `19550dd90` — fail-closed) and one added (`F-019`); nothing in the file records which change was whose |
| G2 | **`classes` is per-entry, not per-cell** — `entry_may_take` grants the entry's whole allowed set on **every** matched cell, so a rationale that scopes a class to one cell in prose cannot be honoured by the mechanism. Two live instances: F-016's `BOOT-P31` "class `effects.stderr` only" (M14) and D-3's "`ledger\|currency\|minor-unit-rounding` may not be forgiven by it" (M15). Fix is per-cell classes, or splitting the entry. | `run.rs:681-687` | **OPEN** | as cited |
| G3 | **The `changelog-register` gate proves PRESENCE, never TRUTH.** It checks each entry's `changelog` string appears verbatim (whitespace-normalised) in the newest `## [x.y.z]` section; the judge only checks the key is truthy. Re-run under the gate's own anchoring: all 41 lines present. Three of them are false of the shipping code — see DOC1. **A second audit found the sharpest instance:** `D-3` is the only entry carrying an explicit `changelog_reason: null` **beside a non-null `changelog`** — a state its own `_comment` rule does not describe — and its own AUDIT NOTE concludes the changelog claim *"is not true of the endpoint these three cells exercise today"*. `changelog-register-check.sh` tests **presence, never coherence**, so it cannot see this. | `xtask/src/gates/changelog_register.rs`, `normalize()` at `:699-701`; `run.rs:1152`; `scripts/changelog-register-check.sh`; `accepted-differences.json` entry `D-3` | **OPEN** | as cited |

Nothing is red today because both cells are `SKIP` — but the entries that describe those behaviours do not reach the cells built to demonstrate them. | cells `llm\|responses\|responses\|request\|ok_citation`, `llm\|bedrock\|bedrock\|request\|ok_cachepoint_document` | **OPEN** | cross-checked every `SKIP` cell against `accepted-differences.json` and `accepted-gaps.json`: register `—`, accepted-gaps `—` for both |

The rule to record, in the tree's own words: **A CAPABILITY THAT IS NEVER CONSTRUCTED IS A CAPABILITY THAT DOES NOT SHIP — its tests prove the code works, not that it runs.** A gate is being built for this; `1.6.0-unconstructed-audit.md` is the scratch that will carry it. | new gate, unbuilt | **OPEN** | the three instances: S6 (`signing_with`, 0 non-test callers), M18 (`booked_lines()` → `Vec::new()` in both production impls), `known_missing_forbid` (both auth crates) |

The row is here because the same error is likely elsewhere in prose: **sweep docs, scripts and workflows for every `cargo xtask gate <name>` whose name is not among the 47 registered gates.** | `scripts/public-hygiene-lint.py`; `xtask` gate registry | **OPEN** | `cargo xtask gate public-hygiene-lint --check` → `xtask gate: no registered gate 'public-hygiene-lint'` |

A RED is a bug to fix; an ABSENT-STEP is a check to build. | `scripts/verify-1.6.0-done.sh`, `absent_step()` ~line 344 | **OPEN** | as cited. **Correction to a figure in circulation: the done-oracle's group count is 23, not 24.** `grep -c '^begin_group'` returns 24 because it counts line 93, the `begin_group()` function definition itself; the script's own gating count (`awk '/^begin_group /{n++}'`, with the trailing space) returns 23, `grep -c '^end_group$'` returns 23, and `DONE_GROUPS_DECLARED=23` agrees with the file. There is no missing 24th group |

| G12 | **THE PLANE ABI RIDER IS LANDED — and the correction matters more than the row.** Two claims that sent agents looking were **wrong, and both were the errors this ledger exists to catch.** (1) *"`export_plane_plugin!` does not exist anywhere in the tree"* — **it exists under a different spelling**, `export_plane!` at `plugin-sdk/src/lib.rs:1204`, and `crates/plane-example` has existed since `fa4330876`. **The plane kind already satisfied universal rule (2); the claim was a search for the wrong name.** (2) The reported `export_{auth,store,secret,hook,export}_plugin!` call-site counts of **8/7/2/2/2 are inflated — the true figures are 1/1/1/1/1.** The count included `.claude/worktrees` sibling checkouts and doc mentions, **which is the very mistake the counter had warned every other agent about.** **The REAL gap was the decorative-reference trap:** the example plane held `PlaneHostVtable::EMPTY` and **said so in its own doc** — *"the example calls back into none of its slots"* — it held the table and never called it. Now every dispatch makes five required crossings (`clock_now`, `govern_admit`, `meter_charge`, `cost_reserve`, `cost_settle`), withdrawing any single slot refuses the dispatch **naming that slot**, and the `CostLeaseId` the plugin carries back settles in core's real process-global lease registry. | `crates/plugin-sdk/src/lib.rs:1204`; `crates/plane-example` | **DONE** | commit `4e39e1a11`; RED 0/7 → GREEN 7/7 on a planted defect, plant grepped back both ways. **§11a's "no 0-caller ABI" now has 20 tests.** Consequential staleness: `docs/design/1.6.0-TRACKER.md:171`'s *"Nothing in CI reds on this"* **is now false** — but its retirement grep (`grep -rln PlaneHostVtable crates/busbar-plane-*`) is **still correctly empty**, because that belongs to the request-loop half, which is separate and still open |

Do not read the tree.** Sibling of BR-X1's positive-control rule. | `cargo tree -e features` | **STANDING PRACTICE** | the `teller-waist` false negative, reproduced |

| DOC22 | **AN OWNER QUESTION FROM 2026-09-04 IS STILL UNANSWERED AND IS NOT RE-PARKED HERE — it is already in the queue.** `findings-2026-09-04.md` **F-004** (69 cells, disposition **owner**): `--migrate-config` rewrites pool members to bare names with a `weights:` map, CHANGES grows 9 → 11, and a `TODO(migrate)` block appears. **Both shapes validate on HEAD.** Its question — *"is the new canonical form the 1.6.0 config shape … or must the migrator keep emitting 1.5.5's shape?"* — **has no `accepted-differences.json` entry answering it.** | `testing/shadow-oracle/findings-2026-09-04.md` F-004 | **PARKED-OWNER** | 69 cells |

## ENGINE — production code: dead code, duplication, architecture

**50 rows — 44 `OPEN`, 4 `PARKED-OWNER`, 1 `IN-FLIGHT`, 1 answered.** These are the duplication census's ranked worklist. Its 27th row
(two SSRF guards) closed and is carried as **S3**; its rate-clamp row is carried as **M1**; its
SigV4, `CallerToken` and breaker-clamp rows are **S10**, **S11**, **S12**. Ordered by the census's
own tiers: safety/money first, then structural decisions, then clean folds, then design work.

**The census's headline, for context, not as a row:** the excess is **90,667** production lines
(62,956 → 153,623, 2.44×), every line assigned, both columns balancing with zero unassigned files.
**The owner's hypothesis scored: "54 crates" REFUTED (~3–5%, measured); "major duplication"
confirmed in kind and corrected in size — ~12,000–14,000 lines, not 91,000.** 21,620 lines (23.8%)
have **no v1.5.5 ancestor at all** and sit *outside* the four new planes: the plane host, the kernel
unit loop, the WAL, the OAuth2 AS, the diagnostics catalog. Five subsystems everyone assumed had
ballooned did not — admin **1.21×**, config+validation **1.22×**, governance **1.23×**, ingress
**1.29×**, auth **1.40×**. Those honest negatives are worth as much as the positives: chasing them
would have been wasted work.

## PROVEN CLEAN — negative results, recorded so the ground is not re-walked

**A gap and a success must never be the same output (rule 2), and neither may a success and an
unexamined surface.** These were looked at hard and found sound. Each one is a place an agent
would otherwise spend a day.

| what was checked | verdict | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The `RecordStore` ABI carries a price | **CLEAN — counts only** | `records.rs:620` enforces it **at compile time** |
| `BudgetCell` carries a spend field | **CLEAN — it does not** | read directly |
| `/admin/usage` resolves the dated rate-card history | **CLEAN, and it makes #79's "owed work" sentence STALE** | `service_operations.rs:1471` prices per row via `card_at(row_priced_at_ms(...))`. **Does NOT supersede M18** — different seam, see that row |
| `CachedPrice` is serializable (and so storable) | **CLEAN — no `Serialize`** | read directly |
| The voice `LocalMeteringPort`'s always-zero price reaches a served session | **CLEAN — but by WIRING, not by type.** Carried as **M26**, because that distinction is the finding | both live paths rebind at `mount.rs:719`, `:1403` |

| **C1 — does ANY store row carry a price?** | **NO, and the COMPILER enforces it.** `MeteringDelta`/`MeteringRow` read field by field: **zero amount fields.** Followed to both store implementations — both persist counts keyed by `(key_id, bucket, model, provider, priced_from_ms)`. `records.rs:671-672`: *"no dollar field crosses the store boundary."* **A doctest at `records.rs:622-629` makes `row.plugin_price_nanos` fail to compile.** ⚠ **SCOPE: this is the `RecordStore` ABI. | **C2 — can `CachedPrice` become a bill?** | **NO, and it cannot be made to.** `posting.rs:55` derives only `Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Debug` — **no `Serialize`, no `Deserialize`**, and no persistence site exists. `Posting::priced_nanos` *"does not consult `Self::cached` and there is no arm here that could."* `recompute.rs:632` compares the two and reports a `Divergence::CardSeq` — **a finding, never an input** | derive list + the absent arm |

The one HEAD read is an opening seal, legitimately *of* the head. **⚠ GAP NAMED: no test feeds a deliberately-wrong `card_seq` into a pricing call and asserts the figure is unchanged — the invariant is STRUCTURAL ONLY** | site-by-site classification |

| **C5 — `/admin/usage` and the dated history: THE SPEC'S OWN "OWED WORK" LINE UNDER #79 IS STALE.** | **THE SEAM IS REALLY INSTALLED AND REALLY DATED.** Traced end to end across 8 hops: route → handler → `get_usage` → history taken once → `as_of` validated **before any book is read** → snapshot pinned → `card_at(...)` → the multiply. Installed at `root/kernel.rs:496,:514`, called from `main.rs:1195`, asserted at `root/tests/kernel.rs:546`. **Covered at THREE levels**, one byte-compared. **The residual is M32's granularity, not the flat-pricing defect the spec describes** | the 8-hop trace + 3 test levels |
| **C6 — the voice `LocalMeteringPort` returns `Some(0)` unconditionally. That is M26** | both paths traced to a common funnel |
| **C7 — is `Count` what #81 specifies?** | **EXACTLY.** `i128` mantissa at scale 6; `Count::parse` reads digit bytes with integer arithmetic and **never builds an intermediate double**; the scale discriminator is a `#[serde(default)]` branch with **no rescale**, per #81(c)/(d); `MAX_ACROSS_A_64_BIT_COLUMN` names the **binding** range rather than the `1.7e32` in-memory one, per #81's RANGE correction. **Zero floats in the module, and a gate row proves it** | module read + gate row |
| **C8 — is `Money` exact?** | **YES.** `Money(i128)` at scale 6; accumulates at `EXACT_SCALE = 15` and **projects ONCE**; `to_decimal_string` formats by integer arithmetic over the mantissa digits — *"No float is formatted and none is parsed back, so what a reader sees is what the book holds."* The module's own no-float assertion was checked and is true | `cost/view.rs:80-89`, `:131-141`, `:53-55` |
| **C9 — do planes condition the ledger append on the card?** | **NO — checked for the exact pattern #43 names as the violation.** The card-presence predicates that DO cross the ABI (`pricing_enabled`, `cost_model_unpriced`, `is_unpriced`) are **yes/no and carry no figure**; they gate **ADMISSION**, fail-closed on an unpriced model per #42 — **not the ledger append.** `rate_for` returns `Some(zero)` when the card is absent so a billing-off deployment reads 0 at the VIEW exactly as #42 requires, and **the accrual at `state.rs:893-984` has no card branch in it** | predicate-by-predicate classification |

| **The mcp rig's anti-vacuity engineering** | **REAL, LOAD-BEARING, AND ALL VERIFIED PRESENT:** `MCP_NO_SKIPS=1` turns every skip into a FAIL; a **vacuity gate** fails any client-role scenario recording no satisfied evidence — *"added after `/usr/bin/true` scored 9 PASS of 14"*; `assert_covered` enforces **set equality**, not a floor (*"a floor alone is satisfied by any 30 of 37"*); the negative control derives its attack list from `--list-modes` rather than a hand-typed one; **the referenced baseline does not exist, so nothing is waived**; and *"NOT ARMED, SO NOT RUN"* is a **RED** state. **The a2a rig is comparable** — `MIN_EXPECTED_TESTS = 85` refuses a shrinking registration, `asserted_row_shortfall` refuses a run whose passes asserted nothing, and `cli.py` returns **3, not 0**, when the subject is unreachable: *"This gate REFUSES TO PASS VACUOUSLY."* **The rigs are not the problem. The wiring is** | each mechanism verified present |
| **Two claims in the commissioning brief** | **BOTH FALSE POSITIVES, run down rather than repeated.** (a) *"teller-step rig suites are missing"* — **every suite the gate cites EXISTS IN TREE**, and a replay of `resolve_cell` over the matrix returns **`RESOLVED: 48  UNRESOLVED: 0`**; `git log --diff-filter=D` over every rig directory finds **zero suite deletions**. The premise under that red is stale. (b) *"`absent_step` is in the gate"* — `grep -rn absent_step xtask/` → **zero hits**; it is a shell function in the done-oracle. **The one genuinely missing artefact was a BRIDGE SCRIPT, not a suite, and it was restored three commits before HEAD** (`e8ec4eaee`) | both re-measured |

## BRANCHES — ALL 816 AUDITED, ONE ROW EACH. THE AUDIT IS COMPLETE.

**All eight slices landed. Every branch has a row. None was skipped.**

```
EMPTY      14      adds nothing the trunk lacks
HARVESTED 133      every distinctive symbol present in the trunk
SURVIVOR  669      carries something absent
          ---
          816
```

Per slice — `aa` 4/38/61 · `ab` 2/18/83 · `ac` 0/12/91 · `ad` 2/25/76 · `ae` 1/22/80 ·
`af` 5/15/83 · `ag` 0/2/101 · `ah` 0/1/94.

### ⚖ OWNER RULING: EXACTLY TWO PREFIXES. A BRANCH'S NAME IS ITS VERDICT.

| prefix | means | count |
|---|---|--:|
| **`delete/*`** | **proven safe to delete.** Every distinctive symbol it adds is present in the trunk, verified. | **147** |
| **`todo/*`** | **carries something the trunk lacks.** Names what, so a reader knows why it survives. | **669** |

**Nothing else.** No `verify/*`, no `land/*`, no `keep-*`, no `wip*`.

> **WHY THE RULING EXISTS, and it is itself a finding:** today's `delete/*` prefix **asserts
> something untrue at scale** — `EMPTY` is **0–5 per slice**. A prefix claiming *"safe to delete"*
> over branches carrying live security fixes is **worse than no prefix**, because it invites
> exactly the bulk deletion it appears to authorise.

### ⚠ READ 669 AS AN UPPER BOUND, NOT A MEASUREMENT

Three reasons, all from the slices themselves, and the ledger will not present an inflated number
as a measured one:

1. **The `busbar-unit-*` → `busbar-kernel-*` refold renamed prose test names around behaviour that
   DID land.** A renamed-but-equivalent test reads as absent.
2. **Slice `ag`'s first instrument had a 15% FALSE-SURVIVOR RATE.** One ripgrep pass over an
   18,695-pattern file: ripgrep matches **leftmost-first and reports no overlapping matches**, so a
   short pattern (`https://`) **shadowed every longer literal starting at the same offset** —
   **1,445 of 9,570 candidates read ABSENT while PRESENT.** Caught only by spot-checking a
   "missing" literal that was visibly in the trunk, then rebuilt on an exact token index
   (identifier presence as an O(1) set test — exact, because `\bID\b` matches iff `ID` is a
   maximal `\w` run) plus rarest-token-then-`index()` for literals. **Every `ag` verdict below is
   from the CORRECTED instrument.**
3. **Several slices verified absence by symbol with behaviour only SAMPLED** on the largest
   stacked branches (one has ~450 test names, another ~340).

**Every row therefore carries its VERIFICATION DEPTH**, and a reader must be able to tell a proven
survivor from an unverified one:

* **depth `symbol-complete`** → `delete/*`. Safe.
* **depth `ADJUDICATED`** (250 rows) → the slice **named what the branch carries** and why the
  trunk lacks it. The `carries:` field is that answer.
* **depth `SYMBOL-ONLY — behaviour NOT verified`** (419 rows) → *"a name this branch defines is not
  in the trunk"*, which — as the false-positive tables prove repeatedly — **is also true of
  harvested work that landed under a different name.** Do not delete; **do not schedule as recovery
  either**, until someone reads the trunk's copy.

### ⛔ TWO PRECONDITIONS BEFORE ANY BRANCH IS RENAMED OR DELETED

1. **A BUNDLE NEWER THAN THE AUDIT MUST EXIST — AND IT NOW DOES.**
   `~/Downloads/busbar-all-refs-<today>.bundle`, 65 MB, **2,391 refs**, `git bundle verify` reports
   a complete history. The prior bundle was **Sep 21 and predated everything found today.**
2. **READ BR-B1 AND BR-T1 FIRST.** A **161-file** golden corpus and **142** golden recordings exist
   only on branches, and **a 1.6.0 binary CANNOT regenerate them — 1.5.5 is the reference.**

**Ancestry and patch-id are both useless here** and no future reader should retry them: only
**3 of 819** branches are ancestors of HEAD (the trunk was rebuilt), and the rename waves moved
every path. **Symbols survive renames; paths do not.**

### ⚠ A METHOD NOTE ANYONE RE-RUNNING THIS MUST HAVE

Slice `af` had to re-confirm every hit against **live HEAD**, because other agents were committing
their own audit reports during the run and a naive `git grep` returned hits from
`docs/design/1.6.0-branch-audit-*.md` — **the audit's own output polluting the corpus it was
measuring.** Exclude those files from the trunk corpus.

### THE ADJUDICATED FINDINGS — what the `MERGE` rows actually carry

| ID | finding | where | status | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| BR-B1 | **⛔ A 161-FILE GOLDEN CORPUS THAT CANNOT BE REGENERATED.** The 1.5.5 shadow-oracle midstream-fault goldens exist only on a branch in slice `ab`. A 1.6.0 binary cannot produce them — **1.5.5 is the reference.** Delete the branch and the corpus is gone for good. **Highest deletion risk in the entire 816.** | slice `ab` S10 | **OPEN — KEEP, owner eye before any deletion** | the corpus is 161 files; no 1.6.0 artifact can recreate a 1.5.5 recording |
| BR-S1 | **A REJECTED CONFIG LEAVES ITS PROCESS-WIDE LIMITS INSTALLED.** A config apply that fails to persist installs the limits anyway: `appbuild.rs:474` commits the guard at `:1923` — the last line of `build_app_from_config` — and does not return it, while the admin apply path persists *afterwards*. **THIS IS THE SSRF CLASS EXACTLY: the spelling `InstallGuard::install` IS in the trunk, so a name scan calls it harvested; reading it reverses the answer.** The trunk ships the *sibling* test `..._does_not_rotate_gov_credentials` for the same transaction on a different resource — **so the class was understood and this member was dropped in a fold.** | `crates/busbar-kernel/src/appbuild.rs:474`, `:1923`; `crates/busbar-core-admin/src/v1/json/handlers.rs:1586` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the fix exists on `delete/keep-core-validate`: a `#[must_use] InstalledLimits` moving the commit to `.keep()` after persist **and** swap, plus `test_admin_v1_config_settings_persist_failure_does_not_install_limits` |
| BR-B2 | **The streams plane family is unlanded and these branches are the only copy** — `busbar-plane-streams{,-openai,-gemini,-twilio}`, `busbar-contract-transport`, `api/src/sha256.rs` with RFC-4231 + SigV4 vectors. Largest unlanded body in slice `ab`. **Not optional scenery: #18/#48 make streaming one of the five planes.** Needs a ruling on whether the trunk's `busbar-plane-streaming` supersedes it or whether this is owed work. | slice `ab` S2 | **PARKED-OWNER** | the four crates have no trunk equivalent under those names |
| BR-B3 | **Telemetry export sinks, pulled pending a ruling that never came** — file / webhook / OTLP / Prometheus, including `an_ssrf_refused_target_composes_no_sink` and *"a cloud-metadata target is refused before it can become a sink"*. **The trunk's SSRF guard is intact (`ff9b`, 57 occurrences) — the gap is the sinks, not the guard.** | slice `ab` S3; the pull is recorded at `docs/design/1.6.0-owner-decisions.md:866` | **PARKED-OWNER** | as cited |
| BR-B4 | **The `PluginError`/`ErrorClass` catalog exists in ZERO trunk files — found independently by two slices.** 361 lines in `busbar-contract/src/error.rs` plus `plugin-loader/src/{config,policy}.rs`, bounded at **8 params / 256 codes / 16 locales**, with a `developer_message` / user-template split. **The trunk's only `PluginError` hit is a doc line PROMISING it** (`docs/design/1.6.0-one-face-per-kind.md:121`, describing it as something a future STORE-4 "lands"). So the trunk has the plan and none of the code. Security-relevant on two axes: the bounds (an unbounded operator-supplied catalog is a memory-exhaustion surface) and the disclosure split (what a refusal tells the caller). | slices `ab` S4 and `ac` S2 | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | `git grep -lF ErrorClass HEAD` → 0; `developer_message` → 0; `error_catalog` → 0; `default_locale` → 0; `PluginError` → 1 file, a doc line |
| BR-B5 | **Conformance suites are missing for FIVE plugin kinds.** `plugin-testkit` was deliberately deleted (owner ruling 2026-09-22, recorded in `crates/store-memory/Cargo.toml`; #2/#31 forbid a shared test util between plugins) — **but the re-inlining is incomplete.** Counting `*_conformance` files in the trunk by kind: plane 4 + loader 2, store 2, export 1, and **auth 0, transport 0, unit 0, secret 0, hook 0.** The only copies of those five suites are on these branches. **Salvage as per-crate inlined tests, per the ruling — never as a shared crate.** | slice `ac` S3 | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the per-kind counts as given; `crates/plugin-testkit/` correctly does not exist (0 files under that prefix) |
| BR-B6 | **A cost-model two-projection equality proof, and the invariant *"admission and accrual must read ONE pinned cost-model snapshot per request."*** Directly relevant to M20/M21/M22 — cross-reference before ruling on any of them. | slice `ab` S7 | **OPEN — money-adjacent** | the branch carries both the proof and the invariant as named tests |
| BR-S2 | **UNBOUNDED RECURSION IN THE PLUGIN-PACK SCHEMA RESOLVER — operator-supplied input, and the failure is a stack overflow.** The trunk caps the schema **walk** (`scan` carries `depth` and refuses past `MAX_SCAN_DEPTH = 64`, `pack.rs:321`) but not the schema **resolve**: `resolve_effective` (`pack.rs:218`) takes **no depth parameter** and recurses through `$ref` (`:238`) and once per `allOf` member (`:250`) **without ever returning through `scan`**, so `scan`'s counter does not advance for the whole resolve chain. The `resolving: &mut HashSet<String>` guard only catches a `$ref` that **repeats a name** — a chain of N *distinct* `$defs` repeats nothing. The triggering document is **flat**, so `serde_json`'s own parse-recursion limit does not apply. **An abort with no diagnostic is the one answer a validator must not give.** | `crates/plugin-sdk/src/pack.rs:218,238,250,321`; reached via `--settings-schema-file` | **OPEN — MERGE→`consolidated/1.6.0`** | the fix on `origin/verify/keep-tests-plugins` adds `depth: u32` + `MAX_RESOLVE_DEPTH = 64` to `resolve_effective` and a **200-link non-cyclic** regression test asserting the refusal comes from the resolver's own cap (`err.contains("while resolving")`), not from `scan`'s |
| BR-S3 | **`$BUSBAR_SIGN_KEY`'s hex error ECHOES A NIBBLE OF THE ed25519 SEED.** `hex::FromHexError::InvalidHexCharacter { c, index }` names the offending character and its index, and that character is a nibble of the 32-byte **signing seed**. A packaging run is exactly where that line ends up — a CI log, a pasted bug report. | BR-X1 | **THE POSITIVE-CONTROL RULE PAID FOR ITSELF THREE TIMES IN ONE NIGHT, AND IT BELONGS HERE AS A STANDING PRACTICE.** Three instruments returned confident, wrong answers and **every one was caught only because the auditor ran the check against a case whose answer was already known.** (1) `grep -owF -f` returned **zero** matches for `key_handle` on a 58 MB corpus where `grep -oF` returned **43** — ugrep 7.8.4's `-w` misbehaves at that input size though it is correct on a small file; redone with `-oF`, **seven** symbols moved absent → present. (2) `git diff --pathspec-from-file` is not accepted by this git for `diff`: it exited **129** with a usage message on stderr and **empty stdout**, which read as *"identical to HEAD"* for **all 95 branches** in one slice. (3) A `grep -E 'a\|b'` alternation was spelled wrong and returned a false zero on constant-time credential compare. **Two further instrument failures in a fourth slice:** `rg` in this environment is a **zsh function, not a binary on `PATH`**, so a `#!/bin/bash` helper silently found zero matches for everything and the first pass reported **100% of symbols missing**; and in zsh `"$b:testing/..."` applies the `:t` history modifier and eats the colon, reporting `NO-FILE` for eight branches whose files were plainly present. **THE RULE: a check that reports the same answer for every input has not measured anything. Run it against one case whose answer you already know, every time.** | the audit method itself | **STANDING PRACTICE** | five instrument failures, five known-answer controls, five catches |
